## STATUS 2026-08-07 — ITERATION 156: **THE OUTAGE RECOVERED AND BOTH RESUME-POINT PRs WERE UNWEDGED — BUT THE HEADLINE IS THAT `m-net-effect-proxy-boundary` M1, IMPLEMENTED FAITHFULLY TO ITS QUORUM-REVIEWED DESIGN, *REMOVES* SSRF IP-BLOCKING FOR LITERAL PRIVATE IPs AND REDS FOUR EXISTING TESTS ON CI's OWN POISONED-PROXY LEGS. `D-1` IS NO LONGER AN ABSTRACT TRADE.** Gate 0/1: kill switch armed, billing **CLEAN**, gh account correct, **ZERO** open `[nightly-eval]` alarms (control: **52** in `--state all`, so the zero is a measurement), inbox 4 messages acked individually (never `ack --all` — the outbound-sweep hazard), **no new Mark comment** on `#559` (watermark `2026-08-04T12:16:12Z`). No rotation due (`#559` created `05:08Z` = **07:08 CEST**, after the Monday-07:00 LOCAL boundary; 52 < 80). `dev == origin/dev == 79caa15b3` and the running `SKILL.md` byte-identical to origin (`cmp` silent) — **the 08-03 reconcile has HELD for a SIXTEENTH consecutive iteration**, so Gate 4 wrote IN PLACE (charter/log/dashboard re-confirmed byte-identical to origin immediately before writing). Cheap tell case-correct with control (ITERATION 155 = 1, 154 = 2, rotation invariant 3). Died-mid-flight check on its **seventh** use: `#606`, `#608`, `#545` and `.wt-iter154` — all known. **FIND 1 — ITERATION 155's `workflow_dispatch` LEVER IS HALF A LEVER, AND BOTH HALVES ARE MEASURED.** (a) **It is TIME-SENSITIVE.** Iter-155 fired a dispatch at `21:30Z` and banked the mechanism from `total=0 → total=1`; that run was **STILL `queued` with `jobs=0` seven hours later**, as was `#606`'s `pull_request` run created `17:02Z`. Dispatches I fired at `23:38Z` went `in_progress` with **`jobs=6` within 12 seconds**. So the discriminator is not "did a run get created" but **"did it acquire jobs"** — `total_count` on `actions/runs` says the record exists; only `runs/<id>/jobs` says anything is happening. A wedged run also **cannot be cancelled**: `gh run cancel` returns *"Cannot cancel a workflow run that is completed"* while the runs API simultaneously reports `queued` — an inconsistent server record with no local remedy. (b) **Worse, a dispatch's checks DO NOT SATISFY BRANCH PROTECTION.** All four required contexts (`test`, `lint`, `build`, `docs-gate`) came back **success on the head SHA** via `commits/<sha>/check-runs` (18 and 12 rows) — and `gh pr checks --required` still showed only `docs-gate`, with `mergeStateStatus=BLOCKED`. The PR is gated on the *`pull_request`* check suite, which was the wedged one. **This is rule 3d exactly**: iter-155 predicted the dispatch would unblock the PR, saw a run appear, and banked the mechanism without checking the thing it was for. Note also the inverse of Gate 3b's own warning — here `gh pr view --json statusCheckRollup` **under-reported** (1 and 0 rows against 18 and 12 from the SHA-addressed endpoint), so the aggregate was vacuously *red* rather than vacuously green. **FIND 2 — THE ACTUAL FIX, AND IT NEEDS NO LOCAL CHECKOUT: A TREE-IDENTICAL EMPTY COMMIT VIA THE GIT API.** `POST git/commits` with the branch's **existing tree SHA** and the current head as parent, then `PATCH git/refs/heads/<br>` — this fires a real `pull_request: synchronize`, creating a fresh, *PR-scoped* check suite, while `tree` is provably unchanged (asserted by comparing the new commit's `.tree.sha` to the old). Measured on both branches: `total=0 → total=5`, all four workflows `in_progress` immediately. It touches no working tree, so Critical Principle 0 is not engaged, and squash-merge absorbs the commit. *(zsh footnote, 5th instance of that class: `-f parents[]=…` **glob-expands** — `no matches found` — so the commit was never created and the ref PATCH failed 422 with an empty sha. It failed safe, and the quoted form `-f 'parents[]'=…` works. Rule 3a(i-b), on the exact shape the skill already warns about.)* **FIND 3, THE HEADLINE — THE DESIGN IS FAITHFULLY IMPLEMENTED AND THAT IS THE PROBLEM.** M1 moves IP validation off the preflight and into the **direct** route only; by design the proxy route does *zero* target resolution or validation. Consequence nobody had drawn: a target that is a **literal private IP** (`https://10.0.0.1/…`) is now handed to the proxy **unvalidated**. Measured with a proper negative control (rule 3d) under the exact env `ci.yml` sets on its own test legs (`:89-91`, `:350-352`): `TestNetIPValidation` is **rc=1 with 4 of 7 subtests FAILING on this branch** and **rc=0 with 7 PASS on pristine `dev`** — the only variable is the change. ## STATUS 2026-07-28 — ITERATION 112: **`m-docs-gate-not-required` LANDED END-TO-END (`a3e781b26` + `cdac5bf04`) — the docs build now ACTUALLY gates, and `docs-gate` is a REQUIRED check on `dev`.** #497's two causes are both closed: the docs job was renamed `build` → `docs-build` (killing the collision with `ci.yml`'s required `build` context — now measured `[.check_runs[]|select(.name=="build")]|length == 1`), and `on.pull_request.paths` was dropped so the workflow always triggers, with a `docs-changes` detector (plain `git diff` vs merge base, path list checked in, no new third-party action) plus an always-reporting `docs-gate` whose **mandatory catch-all arm exits 1** — an unknown/empty `docs_changed` fails loudly rather than defaulting. Planner **opus** → executor **`codex:gpt-5.6-sol`** → evaluator **sonnet PASS 88/100 r1, zero blocking**. **BOTH gate branches were OBSERVED green before the protection flip, which is the whole point**: PR #501 proved the build branch (`docs_changed=true`, `docs-build` success, ~18 min) and PR #502 — one file under `design_docs/`, deliberately absent from the path list — proved the branch #501 *could not* exercise and the one that would have wedged every PR in the repo (`docs_changed=false`, `docs-build` **skipped**, `docs-gate` success, **38 s**). The flip used `PATCH .../required_status_checks` (never a full `PUT`) and the whole protection object was diffed: everything outside `required_status_checks` **byte-identical**. **Canary:** PR #502, the one whose build was skipped, then reported `MERGEABLE/CLEAN` under live protection and MERGED — the merge is the proof. **The planner refuted 7 of the controller's premises**, one load-bearing: a job skipped by an `if:` reports **Success**, not Pending, so requiring `docs-build` directly would have made "not checked" indistinguishable from "passed" — recreating the exact bug. It also showed the concurrency rescope was **load-bearing, not polish**: the shared `pages` group had already cancelled four real PR runs inside 60 s. **The judge earned its fee on a guard-of-a-guard**: the drift guard silently skipped non-single-quoted `on.push.paths` entries — and reproducing that surfaced a SECOND, unreported defect, its `found_push` arm being dead code because `while read; done < <(awk …) || {…}` tests the loop's status, not the substitution's. Both fixed, five vectors tabulated, workflow sha256-identical after mutation. Systemic twin filed as **#503** (`ui-build`, same not-required half, live `/ui` npm Dependabot). `metered=$0.00` (codex 99,836 tokens, no dollar figure emitted by the CLI this run).**

## STATUS 2026-07-28 — ITERATION 109: **RED DEV PREEMPTED THE QUEUE (Gate 1) — the docs deploy is FIXED and dev is green again at `4b757f63d` (CI + Build-and-Release + Deploy-Docs all success).** Dependabot #488 bumped `@mermaid-js/layout-elk` `0.1.9 → 0.2.2`; for a **0.x package a "minor" bump IS breaking**, and `@docusaurus/theme-mermaid@3.10.2` declares `peerOptional ^0.1.9` — so `npm install` failed **ERESOLVE** and "Deploy Documentation" had been red on dev ~7h. Fix = pin back **plus** a Dependabot `ignore`, because a revert alone is re-landed by the next Monday batch. **The durable finding is WHY it landed, filed as [#497](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/497)**: the docs `pull_request` gate — whose own comment claims it *"gates dependency bumps that would break docs"* — is **not a required check**, so `gh pr merge --auto` merged #488 and **cancelled the docs run mid-flight** (`completed/cancelled` @ `23c428378`); and `docusaurus-deploy.yml:58` names its job **`build`**, colliding with `ci.yml:315`'s `build`, which is the context branch protection actually requires — so the gate *appears* wired in the checks list while never gating anything. Verified by reproducing CI's exact failing step (`npm install` rc=0) **and** a full `npm run build` rc=0, which also cleared **#490**'s react/recharts bumps whose docs build had **never once run green**. `metered=$0.00`.**

Both binaries were **stale at pick time** (`-42`/`-39` vs `git describe -60`) and rebuilt before any live check, which is the one step that makes the above a measurement. **FIND 1 — THE MACHINERY ALREADY EXISTS AND #590's AUTHOR SAID SO.** `test_body_lowering.go:126-131` scopes #604 out verbatim and calls the fix "a one-case extension of the switch below"; `FoldTestBody` already lowers to an int sentinel with `CheckInfo` ordinals. **FIND 2 — THE ISSUE'S OWN SUGGESTED FIX IS UNIMPLEMENTABLE, AND THAT IS A CLASS.** The fold is **purely syntactic** — `EvaluateNamedTestBodyExprs` prints the folded AST back to SOURCE and re-runs the whole pipeline, so no type information exists at fold time — therefore "conjoin all **bool-valued** expressions" cannot be implemented as written, because nothing at that point knows which expressions are bool. **This is the 2nd instance of a shape the ghost discipline does not cover: an issue's BUG gets verified and its SUGGESTED FIX does not, then the queue row carries that fix verbatim into the design space.** Instance 1 was iteration 154, where `#602`'s suggested pidfile-polling fix was refuted by the same measurement that confirmed its bug (and its stated cause was off by 3×).
